**Kylian Mbappé Lottin**[^cite_note-2] (born 20 December 1998) is a French professional [footballer](10568.md) who plays as a [forward](428681.md) for [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) club [Real Madrid](26413.md) and [captains](633462.md) the [France national team](250197.md). Widely regarded as one of the best players in the world, he is known for his pace, dribbling, and [finishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_association_football_terms#Finish).[^cite_note-6] He has scored over 400 goals in his senior career, being the all-time top goalscorer for [Paris Saint-Germain](357488.md) ([256](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Paris_Saint-Germain_FC_records_and_statistics#All-time_top_scorers)), and the France national team ([67](250197.md#Top_goalscorers)). He is also the [highest-scoring](43255516.md#Overall_top_goalscorers) player in the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) ([22](43255516.md)).[^cite_note-10]

Born in Paris and raised in [Bondy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondy), Mbappé began his senior career in 2015 with [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS_Monaco_FC), where he won the [2016–17 Ligue 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_AS_Monaco_FC_season) title, the club's first league title in 17 years. In 2017, at age 18, he signed for Paris Saint-Germain (PSG) on an eventual permanent [transfer](2643292.md) worth €180 million, making him the second-[most-expensive player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_association_football_transfers) in the world.[^cite_note-11] He won 15 trophies with PSG, including six [Ligue 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_1) titles and four [Coupes de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coupe_de_France), and helped them reach the [Champions League final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_UEFA_Champions_League_final) in 2020. He also finished as the league's [top goalscorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ligue_1_top_scorers) a record six times, and won [Ligue 1 Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_1_Player_of_the_Year) a record five times. In 2024, Mbappé joined Real Madrid on a free transfer. In his debut season, he scored in the [UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_UEFA_Super_Cup) and [FIFA Intercontinental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_FIFA_Intercontinental_Cup) as Madrid won both titles. He won the [European Golden Shoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Golden_Shoe) in 2025. He was the top scorer in [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_La_Liga) in 2025 and 2026, and in the [Champions League](74127997.md) in 2026.

Mbappé made his senior debut for France in 2017. At the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md), he became the second teenager, after [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9), to score in a [World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_finals) and win the World Cup, being honoured as the [World Cup Best Young Player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Best_Young_Player_Award) and [French Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Player_of_the_Year). He helped France win the [UEFA Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_UEFA_Nations_League) in 2021, ranking as [top scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_UEFA_Nations_League_Finals#Awards) in [the finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_UEFA_Nations_League_Finals). At the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md), Mbappé led France to a second consecutive [final](70432805.md), in which he scored a [hat-trick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hattrick_\(football\)). He set the record for [most goals scored in World Cup finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_final_goalscorers) (4), and won the tournament's [Golden Boot](3482503.md#Golden_Boot) and [Silver Ball](3482503.md#Golden_Ball). At the [2026 World Cup](36581929.md), Mbappé won the [Golden Boot](36581929.md#Individual_and_team_awards) as his 10 goals made him the third-highest scorer in a single World Cup, tied with [Gerd Müller](172313.md). Mbappé also became the first player to win multiple [World Cup Golden Boots](3482503.md).[^cite_note-SagarESPN-12]

Mbappé finished as runner-up for [The Best FIFA Men's Player](52821005.md) in [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Best_FIFA_Football_Awards_2022) and finished third in voting for the [2023 Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ballon_d'Or). *[WhoScored](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opta_Sports#Association_football)* rated him as Europe's best-performing player in 2021–22.[^cite_note-\:11-13] He has been the [UEFA Champions League top scorer in a season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_Champions_League_top_scorers#Top_scorers_by_season) twice and was awarded [IFFHS World's Best Top Goal Scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS_World's_Best_Top_Goal_Scorer) twice. He has been named in the [FIFPRO World 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFPRO_World_11) six times and the [IFFHS Men's World Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS_World_Team#Men's_winners) five times. He was awarded the [Golden Boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Boy_\(award\)) in 2017 and the [Kopa Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopa_Trophy) in 2018. During the [2021–22 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_Ligue_1), he became the first player to finish as both the Ligue 1 top scorer and the top assist provider. Mbappé was named one of [*Time*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))'s [100 most influential people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) in the world in 2023 and was listed by *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* as one of the [world's ten highest-paid athletes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_athletes) in 2023 and 2024.

## Early life

Kylian Mbappé Lottin[^cite_note-14] was born on 20 December 1998[^cite_note-15] in the [19th arrondissement of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_arrondissement_of_Paris),[^cite_note-ninjaturtle2-16] and was raised in [Bondy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondy) in the city's northeastern suburbs.[^cite_note-17] His father, [Wilfrid Mbappé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilfrid_Mbapp%C3%A9), is a football coach who is originally from the [Cameroonian](5447.md) island of [Djébalè](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dj%C3%A9bal%C3%A8?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-18] His mother and [agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_agent), [Fayza Lamari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fayza_Lamari), is of Algerian [Kabyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabyle_people) origin and is a former [handball](13730.md) player.[^cite_note-23] Mbappé has a younger brother, [Ethan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethan_Mbapp%C3%A9), who plays football for [Lille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lille_OSC).[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] Their older adopted brother, [Jirès Kembo Ekoko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jir%C3%A8s_Kembo_Ekoko), is a former professional footballer.[^cite_note-26]

As a child, Mbappé went to a private Catholic school in Bondy, where he was considered academically gifted but unruly.[^cite_note-27] At 15, he began taking Spanish lessons, and eventually became fluent in the language.[^cite_note-28] As a youth, Mbappé's favourite team was [Real Madrid](26413.md),[^cite_note-29] and he also supported [AC Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AC_Milan).[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31] His football idols included [Zinedine Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane),[^cite_note-32] [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md),[^cite_note-33] [Neymar](15326144.md),[^cite_note-\:6-34] [Ronaldinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldinho),[^cite_note-35] [Lionel Messi](2150841.md),[^cite_note-\:6-34] [Ronaldo Nazario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)),[^cite_note-36] [Eden Hazard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eden_Hazard)[^cite_note-37] and [Thierry Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Henry).[^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39] In May 2024, days before his transfer to Real Madrid was announced, Mbappé said that he still follows AC Milan, and would like to play for them in the future.[^cite_note-40]

## Club career

### Early career

When Mbappé started his career with [AS Bondy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS_Bondy) at the age of six, he had several coaches, including his father. Eventually, Mbappé moved to the [Clairefontaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INF_Clairefontaine) football academy. His impressive performances led to numerous clubs attempting to sign him, including [Real Madrid](26413.md), [Chelsea](7473.md), [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.), [Manchester City](165813.md) and [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich).[^cite_note-Youth-22] At the age of 11, Real Madrid invited him to train with their under-12 teams.[^cite_note-41] Mbappé was tracked by [Caen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Malherbe_Caen) for three years, but their relegation from [Ligue 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_1) in 2012 and subsequent failure to win promotion meant they could not afford to sign him.[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] At age 14, Mbappé travelled to London on an invitation from Chelsea, where he played a match for their youth team against [Charlton Athletic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlton_Athletic_F.C.).[^cite_note-44]

### Monaco

In July 2013, at the age of 14, Mbappé joined the [youth academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS_Monaco_Reserves_and_Academy) of [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS_Monaco_FC). The club managed to secure a three-year contract with him despite efforts by Real Madrid to sign him.[^cite_note-48]

#### 2015–2017: Breakthrough and league title

In October 2015, Mbappé was brought in by [Leonardo Jardim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Jardim) to be a mainstay in Monaco's [reserve team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_team). However, his level of skill and maturity prompted his promotion to the main squad after three weeks.[^cite_note-49] He made his first-team debut on 2 December, in a 1–1 draw against [Caen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Malherbe_Caen) in [Ligue 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_1). The 16-year-old Mbappé became Monaco's youngest-ever first-team player, breaking the previous record set by [Thierry Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Henry) in 1994.[^cite_note-monacoteen-46][^cite_note-soccerwayperform-50] On 20 February 2016, Mbappé scored his first goal for the first team, becoming the youngest first-team goalscorer in Monaco's history.[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52] On 6 March, he signed his first professional contract, which kept him with Monaco until June 2019.[^cite_note-53] In an interview with [CNN](62028.md), Monaco's vice-president [Vadim Vasilyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadim_Vasilyev_\(businessman\)) said he knew early on that Mbappé was a "phenomenon".[^cite_note-54]

Mbappé scored the first [hat-trick](411541.md) of his first-team career on 14 December 2016, in a 7–0 home win over [Rennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Rennais_F.C.) in the [Coupe de la Ligue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coupe_de_la_Ligue) round of 16.[^cite_note-57] The following February, Mbappé scored his first Ligue 1 hat-trick in a 5–0 win over [Metz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Metz).[^cite_note-58] On 5 March, he reached ten goals in Ligue 1, becoming the youngest player in thirty years to reach the mark.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60]

In the quarter-finals of the Champions League in 2017 against [Borussia Dortmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borussia_Dortmund), Mbappé scored two goals as Monaco won the first leg 3–2.[^cite_note-61] In the second leg, he opened the scoring as Monaco won 3–1 and advanced to the semi-finals.[^cite_note-62] They were eventually eliminated following a 4–1 loss to [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_FC).[^cite_note-63] Mbappé ended the 2016–17 season with 26 goals from 44 matches in all competitions, as Monaco won the Ligue 1 title.[^cite_note-loan-64] He credited fellow Monaco striker [Radamel Falcao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radamel_Falcao) as a key influence on him throughout the season, giving him the space to express himself and teaching him to be "calm" and "serene" during the game.[^cite_note-kuper1-65]

### Paris Saint-Germain

On 31 August 2017, [Paris Saint-Germain](357488.md) (PSG) announced the signing of Mbappé from Monaco on loan with a mandatory purchase option of €180 million.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] According to PSG's assistant sporting director Luis Ferrer, Mbappé's decision to join the club was partly due to a convincing speech given by manager [Unai Emery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unai_Emery) at the Mbappés' home over the summer.[^cite_note-71] The €180 million fee made Mbappé the most expensive teenager ever and the [second-most-expensive player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_association_football_transfers) ever (behind his teammate [Neymar](15326144.md)), and broke the record for the largest transfer within a domestic league.[^cite_note-76] He was given the number 29 shirt upon arrival in Paris.[^cite_note-PSG_number-77]

#### 2017–2021: Ligue 1 Player of the Year award and UEFA Champions League finalist

On 8 September 2017, Mbappé made his PSG debut, scoring a goal in a 5–1 Ligue 1 win over [Metz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metz_FC).[^cite_note-78] During a 3–0 victory over Bayern Munich in the group stage of the Champions League, he set up goals for [Edinson Cavani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edinson_Cavani) and Neymar.[^cite_note-LES-79] On 6 December, the 18-year-old Mbappé scored his tenth career Champions League goal in a 3–1 loss to Bayern, becoming the youngest player to reach that mark.[^cite_note-CL_record-80] In July 2018, Mbappé was given the number 7 shirt for the [upcoming season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Paris_Saint-Germain_FC_season) with PSG.[^cite_note-number_7-81]

In his first appearance of the season, Mbappé scored twice in the last 10 minutes as PSG won 3–1 against [Guingamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/En_Avant_de_Guingamp) in Ligue 1.[^cite_note-82] On 1 September, he scored and assisted in a 4–2 win over [Nîmes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%AEmes_Olympique), but was [sent off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sent_off_\(association_football\)) for the first time in his career following an altercation with [Téji Savanier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A9ji_Savanier).[^cite_note-83] The following month, Mbappé scored four goals within 13 minutes in a 5–0 victory over [Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympique_Lyonnais).[^cite_note-84] In December, he was the inaugural winner of the [Kopa Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kopa_Trophy), which is awarded by *[France Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Football)* to the best under-21 player in the world.[^cite_note-85] On 19 January 2019, Mbappé scored a hat-trick in a 9–0 win over Guingamp.[^cite_note-86] On 2 March, he scored twice in a 2–1 win over Caen to reach his 50th goal with the club.[^cite_note-87] PSG finished the season as Ligue 1 champions,[^cite_note-ligue1history-88] with Mbappé winning the [Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_1_Player_of_the_Year) award[^cite_note-UNFP-89] and finishing the season as the [top goalscorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Ligue_1#Top_goalscorers) with 33 goals.[^cite_note-ligue1history-88]



Mbappé in a pre-season friendly match against [Dynamo Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamo_Dresden) in July 2019

On 3 August 2019, Mbappé scored in a 2–1 win over Rennes to capture his first title of the 2019–20 season, the [Trophée des Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Troph%C3%A9e_des_Champions).[^cite_note-2019TDC-90] On 22 October, he scored a [perfect hat-trick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perfect_hat-trick) in a 5–0 victory over [Club Brugge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Brugge_KV) in the Champions League.[^cite_note-91] On 1 May 2020, PSG were crowned Ligue 1 champions after the season was cut short during the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_France).[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] Mbappé finished the 2019–20 season as the joint-top scorer in Ligue 1 with 18 goals, along with Monaco's [Wissam Ben Yedder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wissam_Ben_Yedder); Mbappé was assigned the title due to his higher goals-per-game ratio.[^cite_note-2019–20_Ligue_1_top_scorer-94] After an injury during the [Coupe de France final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Coupe_de_France_final), Mbappé was unable to play in the [Coupe de la Ligue final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Coupe_de_la_Ligue_final),[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96] which PSG went on to win, completing a domestic [treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)).[^cite_note-97] He returned to action in the Champions League quarter-final against [Atalanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atalanta_BC) on 12 August, assisting a match-winning goal in injury time.[^cite_note-98] PSG lost 1–0 to Bayern Munich in the [Champions League final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_UEFA_Champions_League_final) on 23 August.[^cite_note-CL_2020-99]

Mbappé missed the first three matches of the season after contracting [COVID-19](63030231.md).[^cite_note-\:2-100] He returned on 20 September 2020, scoring a goal in a 3–0 win against [Nice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OGC_Nice).[^cite_note-101] In a Ligue 1 match against [Montpellier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montpellier_HSC) on 5 December, Mbappé became the fifth player to score 100 goals for PSG.[^cite_note-102] During the Champions League group stage, Mbappé scored two goals in a 5–1 victory over [İstanbul Başakşehir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba%C5%9Fak%C5%9Fehir_F.K.), qualifying PSG for the [knockout phase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_UEFA_Champions_League_knockout_phase).[^cite_note-103] He became the youngest player in Champions League history to reach twenty goals, although his record was surpassed by [Erling Haaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erling_Haaland) later in the season.[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] On 16 February 2021, a [Champions League hat-trick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_Champions_League_hat-tricks) saw Mbappé become PSG's [third-highest scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Paris_Saint-Germain_FC_records_and_statistics#All-time_top_scorers).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] On 10 March, Mbappé's goal in a 1–1 draw against Barcelona saw him surpass [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) as the youngest player to reach 25 Champions League goals.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109]

In the first leg of the Champions League quarter-final against Bayern Munich on 7 April, Mbappé scored two goals in PSG's 3–2 victory.[^cite_note-110] PSG was eliminated in the semi-finals.[^cite_note-111] On 19 May, Mbappé had one goal and one assist as PSG won the [Coupe de France final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Coupe_de_France_final) against Monaco, claiming their first major trophy of the season.[^cite_note-112] He ended the Ligue 1 campaign with 27 goals, becoming the top scorer for the third consecutive season.[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114] He also received the Ligue 1 Player of the Year award and was included in the Ligue 1 Team of the Season.[^cite_note-115]

#### 2021–2023: Contract extension and PSG all-time top goalscorer



Left to right: Mbappé, [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) and [Neymar](15326144.md) playing for [Paris Saint-Germain](357488.md) in 2021

On 14 August 2021, prior to PSG's opening home match of the [2021–22 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_Paris_Saint-Germain_FC_season) against [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RC_Strasbourg_Alsace), Mbappé was booed by spectators amidst rumours that he wanted to sign for the [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) club Real Madrid.[^cite_note-116] Mbappé scored his first goal of the season in a 4–2 win against [Brest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Brestois_29) six days later.[^cite_note-117] On 7 December, he scored two goals in a 4–1 Champions League win over [Club Brugge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Brugge_KV), making him the youngest player in history to reach both 30 and 31 goals in the Champions League.[^cite_note-118] On 12 December, the 22-year-old Mbappé scored twice against Monaco in Ligue 1, becoming the youngest player to score 100 goals for a single top-level French team since [Opta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opta_Sports) began recording data during the [1950–51 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950%E2%80%9351_French_Division_1).[^cite_note-119] During the Champions League round of 16, Mbappé became the second-highest goalscorer in PSG history.[^cite_note-123]

Despite speculations of a possible transfer to Real Madrid, Mbappé extended his contract with PSG until 2024, with a one-year optional extension.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] According to [Sky Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Sports), Mbappé's contract included a monthly wage of £4 million, making him the highest-paid player in the world.[^cite_note-\:1-126] He also reportedly received a signing-on figure in the region of £100 million.[^cite_note-\:1-126] With 28 goals during the season, Mbappé finished as the top scorer for the fourth consecutive time.[^cite_note-127] He also provided 17 assists in the season, making him the first player to finish as both top scorer and top assist provider in the league's history.[^cite_note-\:3-128][^cite_note-\:4-129]

On 11 October 2022, Mbappé scored a penalty kick in a 1–1 Champions League draw against [Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica), becoming the leading goalscorer for PSG in European competitions with 31 goals.[^cite_note-130] The match occurred in the midst of media reports that Mbappé wanted to leave PSG during the January [transfer window](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfer_window), which he denied.[^cite_note-131] On 23 January 2023, Mbappé became the first PSG player to score five goals in a match, including a ten-minute hat-trick, in a 7–0 victory over [Pays de Cassel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Pays_de_Cassel) in the Coupe de France round of 32.[^cite_note-132] In early March, he scored his 201st goal for PSG in a 4–2 win over Nantes, becoming the club's all-time leading scorer.[^cite_note-133] He ended the Ligue 1 campaign with 29 goals, becoming the top scorer for the fifth consecutive season as PSG won their record 11th Ligue 1 title.[^cite_note-2022–23_Ligue_1_top_scorer-134][^cite_note-DN-135] He received the Ligue 1 Player of the Year award for the fourth consecutive season and was named in the Ligue 1 Team of the Season.[^cite_note-23LPY-136][^cite_note-TOTY23-137]

#### 2023–2024: Final season

On 13 June 2023, via an official statement released through *[Agence France-Presse](471981.md)*, Mbappé announced his decision not to renew his contract with PSG, which was set to expire in June 2024. According to him, the club had been informed about his decision since July 2022.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] In response to an article published by *[Le Parisien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Parisien)* about his reported desire to join Real Madrid in the upcoming summer window, Mbappé [tweeted](9988187.md) that the rumours were "lies", and reaffirmed his intention to continue at PSG.[^cite_note-140] However, during the unveiling of new manager [Luis Enrique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Enrique) on 5 July, PSG president [Nasser Al-Khelaifi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Al-Khelaifi) stated that if Mbappé wanted to stay, he would have to "sign a new contract".[^cite_note-141] Mbappé was subsequently excluded from PSG's preseason tour in Japan.[^cite_note-145] After much speculation surrounding his future at PSG, a statement from the club on 13 August confirmed his reintegration into the first team.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147]

On 26 August, Mbappé scored his 150th Ligue 1 goal for PSG in a 3–1 win over [Lens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RC_Lens).[^cite_note-148] On 11 November, he scored his first hat-trick of the season in a 3–0 win over Reims, lifting PSG to the top of the Ligue 1 standings.[^cite_note-149] On 3 January 2024, Mbappé scored the second goal in a 2–0 victory over Toulouse in the [2023 Trophée des Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Troph%C3%A9e_des_Champions), securing his first trophy of the season. In the process, he became the all-time top goalscorer at the [Parc des Princes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parc_des_Princes), with 111 goals.[^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151] Four days later, Mbappé scored a hat-trick in a 9–0 win over [Revel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Revel) in the round of 64 of the Coupe de France, becoming the club's all-time top scorer in the competition by bringing his goal tally to 30.[^cite_note-152]

Mbappé scored his second hat-trick of the season on 19 March in a 6–2 win over Montpellier, bringing him to 250 goals in all competitions for PSG.[^cite_note-153] On 10 May, he announced on social media that he would not renew his contract, and would depart PSG at the end of the season.[^cite_note-154] In his final match with the club on 25 May, he secured a trophy as PSG defeated Lyon in the [Coupe de France final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Coupe_de_France_final).[^cite_note-155] He concluded his final season with PSG as the [Champions League joint top scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_Champions_League_top_scorers#Top_scorers_by_season) with 8 goals, tied with [Harry Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Kane).[^cite_note-auto2-156]

### Real Madrid

On 3 June 2024, La Liga club Real Madrid announced that Mbappé had signed a five-year contract with the club, concluding a highly anticipated seven-year transfer saga.[^cite_note-\:8-157][^cite_note-\:9-158] His move to Madrid had been the subject of intense speculation and negotiations, reflecting his status as one of football's most sought-after players.[^cite_note-159] After signing he stated: "It's a dream come true. So happy and proud to join the club of my dreams."[^cite_note-160] On 16 July, Mbappé was unveiled as a Real Madrid player at the [Santiago Bernabéu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernab%C3%A9u_\(stadium\)), wearing the number 9 shirt. His presentation was attended by 80,000 spectators.[^cite_note-166]

#### 2024–2025: Debut season goalscoring records and European Golden Shoe



Mbappé playing for [Real Madrid](26413.md) in 2025

Mbappé made his debut for Real Madrid on 14 August 2024 in the [2024 UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_UEFA_Super_Cup) against Atalanta, scoring the second goal of a 2–0 win.[^cite_note-167] He debuted in La Liga on 18 August, during a 1–1 draw against [Mallorca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCD_Mallorca).[^cite_note-168] On 1 September, he scored his first two La Liga goals in Madrid's 2–0 victory over [Real Betis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Betis).[^cite_note-169] Later in the month, he scored his first Champions League goal for the club during their 3–1 victory over [VfB Stuttgart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VfB_Stuttgart) on the inaugural matchday of the newly formatted [Champions League league phase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_UEFA_Champions_League_league_phase).[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] On 10 December, Mbappé scored his 50th Champions League goal in a 3–2 victory against Atalanta, becoming the second-youngest player to reach the mark after Lionel Messi.[^cite_note-172]

On 25 January 2025, Mbappé scored his first hat-trick for Madrid in a 3–0 victory over league opponents [Real Valladolid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Valladolid).[^cite_note-173] With five league goals in January, he was named [La Liga Player of the Month](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga_Player_of_the_Month).[^cite_note-auto-174] On 19 February, he recorded his first hat-trick for Madrid in the Champions League in a 3–1 win over Manchester City, securing the team's progression to the [Champions League round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_UEFA_Champions_League_knockout_phase).[^cite_note-175] On 13 April, he received his first red card with Madrid after making a controversial tackle on [Antonio Blanco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Blanco_\(footballer\)), which he was widely scrutinised for.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177] On 11 May, Mbappé's hat-trick in an *[El Clásico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cl%C3%A1sico)* loss to Barcelona brought him to 39 goals for Madrid, breaking [Iván Zamorano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Zamorano)'s record for most goals scored by a Real Madrid player in their debut season.[^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179] In his next game, Mbappé scored his 28th league goal of the season, setting a record for most goals for Real Madrid in a debut season in La Liga.[^cite_note-180] Mbappé finished his La Liga campaign with 31 goals in 34 games, winning the [Pichichi Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pichichi_Trophy) and the [European Golden Shoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Golden_Shoe) for the first time in his career.[^cite_note-24-25-goalscoring-181] On 28 May, he was named Madrid's Player of the Season.[^cite_note-RealMadridPOTS-182]

#### 2025–2026: La Liga and Champions League top scorer

For the [2025–26 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Real_Madrid_CF_season), Mbappé switched to the number 10 shirt, which became available after the departure of midfielder [Luka Modrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Modri%C4%87).[^cite_note-183] On 16 September, he scored his 49th and 50th goals for Madrid against Marseille in a 2–1 Champions League win, becoming the fastest player to achieve this feat for Madrid since [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md).[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] Late in November, Mbappé scored four goals in a 4–3 win over [Olympiacos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympiacos_F.C.), including the second-fastest hat-trick in Champions League history, completed in six minutes and 42 seconds.[^cite_note-186] On 20 December, he scored in a 2–0 victory over [Sevilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sevilla_FC), equalling Cristiano Ronaldo's record of 59 goals in a calendar year for Madrid.[^cite_note-187] He concluded the season by securing his second Pichichi Trophy with 25 [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_La_Liga) goals,[^cite_note-188] while also finishing as the top scorer in the [UEFA Champions League](74127997.md) with 15 goals.[^cite_note-189]

#### 2026–2027 season

On 27 August 2026, Mbappé scored his sixth hat-trick for Real Madrid during a 4–1 victory over [Real Sociedad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Sociedad).[^cite_note-190] On 8 September, he scored against [Inter Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter_Milan) in a 2–1 win, equalling [Raúl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl) as the fifth top scorer in Champions League history, with 71 career goals.[^cite_note-191] Leading up to a match between Madrid and [Elche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elche_CF) on 15 September, the two clubs agreed that their players would wear shirts bearing a message of support for the inhabitants of [Ceuta](6443.md), a North African autonomous city controlled by Spain. In July 2026, more than 70,000 migrants [illegally entered](83824713.md) the city from neighboring Morocco, leading to a humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-192] Mbappé and his teammates [Vinícius Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin%C3%ADcius_J%C3%BAnior) and [Ibrahima Konaté](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahima_Konat%C3%A9) wore the shirts partially rolled up, obscuring part of the message, and removed them before the pre-match handshake. After facing criticism in the Spanish press and on social media, Mbappé explained that he wore the shirt rolled up because he wanted to express solidarity not only with Ceuta but also with migrants who had died or were living in difficult conditions.[^cite_note-196]

## International career

### 2014–2018: Youth level and senior debut

In July 2016, Mbappé scored five times for [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_national_under-19_football_team) as they won the [2016 UEFA European Under-19 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_UEFA_European_Under-19_Championship). His two goals in a 3–1 victory over [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal_national_under-19_football_team) in the semi-finals had helped his team reach the final.[^cite_note-monacoteen-46] He was included in [the competition's Team of the Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_UEFA_European_Under-19_Championship#Team_of_the_Tournament). Mbappé received his first call-up to the [French senior squad](250197.md) to face [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_national_football_team) on 25 March 2017.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198] On 31 August, Mbappé scored his first senior international goal in a 2018 World Cup qualification match against the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_national_football_team).[^cite_note-199]

### 2018–2021: FIFA World Cup triumph



Mbappé holding the [FIFA World Cup Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Trophy) after [France](250197.md) won the [2018 World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_final)

Mbappé was called up to the [France squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_squads#France) for the [2018 World Cup](13327177.md) in Russia.[^cite_note-200] On 21 June, the 19-year-old Mbappé scored his first World Cup goal in a 1–0 [group stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_C) win over [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru_national_football_team), which made him the youngest French goalscorer in World Cup history.[^cite_note-201] On 30 June, he scored two goals and was named [man of the match](906698.md) in a [4–3 win](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage#France_vs_Argentina) over [Argentina](454699.md) in the round of 16.[^cite_note-202] Mbappé was the second teenager to score two goals in a World Cup match after [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9) in [1958](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup).[^cite_note-203] In a post-match press conference, he stated: "It's flattering to be the second one after Pelé but let's put things into context – Pelé is in another category."[^cite_note-Pelé_1958-204] On 15 July, Mbappé scored a goal in the [World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_final) as France defeated [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team) 4–2.[^cite_note-205] He became the second teenager, after Pelé, to [score in a World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_final_goalscorers). Having scored four goals in the tournament, he received the [FIFA World Cup Best Young Player Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Best_Young_Player_Award).[^cite_note-206] Pelé congratulated him on social media.[^cite_note-207]

On 11 June 2019, Mbappé scored his 100th career goal in a 4–0 win over [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra_national_football_team) in a qualifier for [UEFA Euro 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020).[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] However, the tournament was postponed for a year due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-210] In September 2020, Mbappé tested positive for [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_disease_2019), which sidelined him for a month.[^cite_note-213] On 18 May 2021, he was included in [France's squad for Euro 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020_squads#France), his second major international tournament.[^cite_note-214] France made it to the [Euro 2020 round of 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020_knockout_phase#Round_of_16), where a 3–3 draw against [Switzerland](681862.md) went to a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md); Mbappé failed to score the decisive fifth penalty kick, and France was eliminated from the tournament.[^cite_note-215]

### 2021–2023: Nations League title and second consecutive World Cup final



Mbappé playing for [France](250197.md) at the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md)

In the [UEFA Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Nations_League) semi-final on 7 October 2021, Mbappé contributed a goal and an assist as France defeated [Belgium](679716.md) 3–2. It was his 50th appearance for the French national team.[^cite_note-216] Three days later, in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_UEFA_Nations_League_final) against [Spain](609825.md), Mbappé scored the winning goal to give France a 2–1 victory and their first Nations League title.[^cite_note-217] With two goals and two assists in the Nations League Finals, Mbappé was awarded the competition's Golden Boot, known as the "[Alipay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alipay) Top Scorer Trophy."[^cite_note-2021_Nations_League_Top_Scorer-218]

On 13 November 2021, Mbappé scored four times in an 8–0 win over [Kazakhstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazakhstan_national_football_team) during the [2022 World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification).[^cite_note-219] It was his first hat-trick in an international match, as well as the first competitive hat-trick for France since 1985.[^cite_note-220] During the group stage of the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md) in Qatar, Mbappé scored both of France's goals in a 2–1 victory over [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark_national_football_team), securing entry to the knockout stages and breaking the "[World Cup Champions' Curse](11347008.md#European_World_Cup_champions'_curse)".[^cite_note-221]

During the [World Cup final](70432805.md) on 18 December 2022, Mbappé became the second player in history to score a hat-trick in a World Cup final.[^cite_note-222] Argentina had a 2–0 lead over France with just over ten minutes remaining, before Mbappé scored twice in two minutes. In [extra time](633242.md), Argentina took the lead again, before Mbappé scored a penalty kick to even the score at 3–3. The match went to a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md); Mbappé scored his penalty, but France lost the shoot-out.[^cite_note-223]

Mbappé won the tournament's [Golden Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Boot) with eight goals, and became the sixth all-time top goalscorer in World Cup history with twelve goals across two World Cups, tied with Pelé.[^cite_note-224] Having scored a goal in the 2018 final, Mbappé also became the top goalscorer in World Cup finals, with four goals.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] Mbappé won the Silver Ball as the second-best player of the tournament,[^cite_note-FIFAGoldenBall-227] and completed the most dribbles throughout the tournament (25).[^cite_note-228]

### 2023–2025: Captaincy and Euro 2024 semi-finals

On 21 March 2023, following the international retirement of [Hugo Lloris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Lloris), France manager [Didier Deschamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didier_Deschamps) named Mbappé the team's new [captain](633462.md) ahead of their [UEFA Euro 2024 qualifying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024_qualifying) campaign.[^cite_note-229] In his first match as captain, Mbappé led France to a 4–0 victory over the Netherlands, scoring two goals and assisting another.[^cite_note-230] On 19 June, Mbappé scored his 40th goal for France and became the youngest player to make 70 appearances for the team.[^cite_note-231] On 13 October, he scored two goals in a 2–1 win over the Netherlands, securing qualification for [UEFA Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024).[^cite_note-232] On 18 November, he scored a [hat-trick](411541.md) and provided three assists in a [14–0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_v_Gibraltar_\(UEFA_Euro_2024_qualifying\)) victory over [Gibraltar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibraltar_national_football_team), France's largest-ever victory.[^cite_note-233] His three goals brought him to 300 total career goals and 46 goals for France, making him France's third all-time top scorer.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235]

In France's final group-stage match of Euro 2024, Mbappé scored his first-ever goal at the European Championships during a 1–1 draw against Poland, which allowed France to advance to the knockout stages.[^cite_note-236] In the semi-finals, he set-up [Randal Kolo Muani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randal_Kolo_Muani)'s opening goal in an eventual 2–1 defeat to Spain.[^cite_note-237] Following France's elimination from the tournament, Mbappé described his performance as a "failure".[^cite_note-238]

On 8 June 2025, Mbappé scored his 50th goal for France during a 2–0 victory over Germany in the [2025 UEFA Nations League Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_UEFA_Nations_League_Finals) third place play-off.[^cite_note-239] On 9 September, he became France's second all-time top goalscorer when he scored his 52nd international goal in a 2–1 victory over Iceland during a [qualifying match for the 2026 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_D).[^cite_note-244] On 13 November, Mbappé scored twice in a 4–0 win against [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_national_football_team), reaching 400 career goals and helping his country secure qualification for the [World Cup](36581929.md).[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] Mbappé is the youngest player to score 400 career goals since [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9) in 1964.[^cite_note-247]

### 2026–present: Third World Cup and France all-time top goalscorer



Mbappé with [France](250197.md) at the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md)

In France's opening match of the 2026 World Cup, Mbappé scored two goals in a 3–1 win over [Senegal](1017436.md), surpassing [Olivier Giroud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Giroud) to become France's all-time top scorer. With 14 World Cup goals, Mbappé surpassed [Just Fontaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_Fontaine)'s record for the most career World Cup goals scored by a French player.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] On 22 June, Mbappé made his 100th international appearance for France,[^cite_note-250] scoring two goals in a 3–0 win over [Iraq](1039652.md).[^cite_note-251] He became the first player to score two or more goals in six different World Cup matches.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] He then scored twice in a 3–0 victory against [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_men's_national_football_team) in the first knockout round, becoming the all-time leading goalscorer in World Cup knockout stages with ten goals, which broke the previous record held by [Leônidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le%C3%B4nidas_\(footballer,_born_1913\)) and [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)).[^cite_note-254] He also surpassed [Miroslav Klose](201840.md) to become the second-highest all-time World Cup goalscorer, with 18 goals, behind [Lionel Messi](2150841.md).

On 4 July, Mbappé scored from a penalty kick in a 1–0 win against [Paraguay](804025.md) in the round of 16, which was his seventh goal of the tournament.[^cite_note-255] After the match, Paraguayan senator [Celeste Amarilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celeste_Amarilla) made comments about Mbappé on social media which were widely condemned as racist. Mbappé called Amarilla "despicable", and the [French Football Federation](682002.md) announced that it would file a criminal complaint with the [French Public Prosecutor's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minist%C3%A8re_public_\(France\)). French prosecutors subsequently opened an investigation into the alleged racist abuse.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257]

In France's 2–0 quarter-final win over [Morocco](1024865.md), Mbappé scored once and provided an assist for [Ousmane Dembélé](48621084.md)'s goal, making him the first player to record ten [goal contributions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goal_contribution) at each of two separate World Cups.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259] France's 2–0 loss to [Spain](609825.md) in the semi-final was Mbappé's 21st career World Cup match, which saw him surpass [Hugo Lloris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Lloris) to become the France player with the most World Cup appearances.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] He scored two goals and provided an assist in a 6–4 loss to [England](9904.md) in the third-place match, bringing his tallies to 10 goals in the tournament and 22 in his World Cup career, which saw him overtake Messi as the all-time World Cup top goalscorer.[^cite_note-SagarESPN-12][^cite_note-262] He was awarded the [Golden Boot](36581929.md#Awards) as the top scorer at the World Cup for the second consecutive time, becoming the first player to win the award twice, and won the Bronze Ball as the third-best player of the tournament.[^cite_note-FIFA2026Awards-263][^cite_note-264] He also became the third-highest scorer in a single World Cup, behind [Sándor Kocsis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1ndor_Kocsis) and [Just Fontaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_Fontaine), while his 10 goals and four assists set a new record for the most goal contributions in a single World Cup since record-keeping began in 1966.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266]

## Player profile

### Style of play

A versatile forward, Mbappé often plays as a [winger](428681.md#Winger). He is capable of playing on either flank, due to his skill with both feet;[^cite_note-better_than_Henry-267][^cite_note-268] he can cut into the centre onto his stronger right foot from the left wing, or create chances and provide assists for teammates from the right thanks to his vision.[^cite_note-273] He is also able to play in the [centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-forward) as a main [striker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striker_\(association_football\)) due to his composure, clinical finishing, and eye for goal.[^cite_note-278]

Mbappé is known for his [dribbling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dribbling_\(association_football\)) ability, as well as his acceleration, agility, quick feet, first touch, and link-up play. He is also known for his creativity when in possession of the ball. He is known to make elaborate feints, such as [step overs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Step_over) or sudden changes of pace or direction, to beat opponents in one-on-one situations.[^cite_note-282] He is also highly regarded for his pace and close ball control when dribbling at speed. This, along with his movement and tactical intelligence, allows him to beat the defensive line by making attacking runs into space both on and off the ball.[^cite_note-wonderkid-269][^cite_note-world_at_his_feet-280] His ability to time his runs enables him to stretch opposing defences, making him a dangerous offensive threat on counter-attacks.[^cite_note-285]

Since the early years of his career, Mbappé has been compared to great players in the history of football. [Arsène Wenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ars%C3%A8ne_Wenger) said Mbappé had similar qualities to Thierry Henry.[^cite_note-286] Mbappé's talent and performances for France at the 2018 World Cup led him to be compared to Pelé in the media.[^cite_note-287] Former France international player [Nicolas Anelka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Anelka) said that Mbappé's ability to run at defences reminded him of [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\))'s performances at the [1996 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1996_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_tournament).[^cite_note-288]

### Reception

Mbappé is widely regarded as one of the best players of his generation[^cite_note-291] and currently one of the best players in the world.[^cite_note-\:7-4][^cite_note-\:10-5] [Jean-Pierre Papin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Pierre_Papin) described him as the greatest striker in the history of the France national team.[^cite_note-292] Writing for the official Olympics website in 2026, Courtney Hill said that Mbappé "comfortably slots into debates about France’s greatest-ever players."[^cite_note-\:102-293] Following Mbappé's performance at the 2026 World Cup, former England player [Peter Crouch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Crouch) described him as "potentially the greatest ever World Cup player",[^cite_note-\:03-294] while *[The Athletic](54936152.md)* called him "unstoppable, a formidable blend of speed, strength and skill", who appeared to score "at will".[^cite_note-\:03-294] The Dutch player [Stefan de Vrij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_de_Vrij) described Mbappé as his toughest opponent.[^cite_note-295]

In 2018, Mbappé was considered the world's most expensive player from a transfer value perspective by the [CIES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Centre_for_Sports_Studies).[^cite_note-296] In 2020, he was named the fastest player in the world by the French newspaper *[Le Figaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Figaro)*.[^cite_note-297] His performances at the club and international level saw him shortlisted for the [Laureus World Sportsman of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureus_World_Sports_Award_for_Sportsman_of_the_Year) award in 2019[^cite_note-298] and 2023.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] In 2023, Mbappé was included on the *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100)* list of the 100 most influential people in the world.[^cite_note-301]

## Sponsorships and wealth

Mbappé signed a sponsorship deal with [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) when he was 9 years old.[^cite_note-302] In 2017, Nike began producing his signature football boots, the Kylian Mbappé Nike Hypervenom 3.[^cite_note-303] In 2018, Mbappé unveiled the Nike Mercurial Superfly VI boots, which were inspired by the R9 Mercurial boots of former Brazilian striker [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)).[^cite_note-304] The same year, the Swiss watchmaker [Hublot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hublot) signed Mbappé as a [brand ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brand_ambassador).[^cite_note-305] In 2021, Mbappé became a brand ambassador for [Dior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dior), a partnership that was initially announced for a two-year period but is still ongoing as of 2025.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] In 2022, Mbappé signed a multi-year sponsorship agreement with the sunglasses and sporting goods manufacturer [Oakley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakley,_Inc.).[^cite_note-308] In 2026, he terminated his sponsorship deal with Nike and signed a deal with the sportswear company [On](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_\(company\)).[^cite_note-309]

In 2023, Mbappé was ranked third on the [*Forbes* list of the world's highest-paid athletes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_athletes).[^cite_note-310] *Forbes* ranked him sixth on the list in 2024 and 16th in 2025.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] *[Sportico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penske_Media_Corporation#Sportico)* listed him as the fifth highest-paid athlete of 2023 and the ninth highest-paid athlete of 2024.[^cite_note-317]

## In popular culture

### Depictions in art

Mbappé has been the subject of [street art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_art) and wax sculpture. A large [mural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural) depicting him was painted in 2018 in his hometown of [Bondy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondy),[^cite_note-318] and a second, Nike-commissioned mural by artist [Akiko Stehrenberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akiko_Stehrenberger) from GlobalStreetArt was unveiled there in late 2019 to mark Mbappé's 21st birthday.[^cite_note-319] Mbappé has been rendered as a [wax figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wax_figure) at the [Musée Grévin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_Gr%C3%A9vin) in Paris,[^cite_note-320] and at [Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds) Berlin[^cite_note-321] and London. The London figure is displayed with items Mbappé donated, including his France jersey and his captain's armband.[^cite_note-322]

### Media and Internet culture

In 2023, Mbappé was part of [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix)'s docuseries *[Captains of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captains_of_the_World)*, which followed national team captains during the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md).[^cite_note-323] Ahead of the [UEFA Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024), [BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport) produced the documentary *Mbappé*, tracing his rise from the [Paris suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banlieue) to [World Cup](11370.md) glory.[^cite_note-324] In 2021, he published the novel *Je m'appelle Kylian* with the French cartoonist Faro, which is aimed at young readers.[^cite_note-325] In 2024, Mbappé and his production company *Zebra Valley* were reportedly working on a [behind-the-scenes-documentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Behind-the-scenes) featuring his transfer to [Real Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid).[^cite_note-326]

Mbappé has generated sustained attention in [internet meme culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meme_culture). Early in his career, he was frequently depicted as a ninja turtle because of a perceived resemblance to the characters from *[Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teenage_Mutant_Ninja_Turtles)*.[^cite_note-ninjaturtle2-16] Particularly from 2026 onward, memes portraying him as a "[dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictator)" emerged following rumours of his influence over decisions at both the club and national team levels.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

### Video games

Mbappé has been featured in EA Sports' [*FIFA*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_\(video_game_series\)) and [*FC*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports_FC) video game series.[^cite_note-329] His trademark [goal celebration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goal_celebration) – posing with his arms folded and hands tucked under his armpits – was inspired by his younger brother Ethan, who would celebrate in this manner when beating Kylian at *FIFA* video games.[^cite_note-333] Mbappé appeared on the cover of *[FIFA 21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_21)*, *[FIFA 22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_22)*, *[FIFA 23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_23)* and *[EA Sports FC 27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports_FC_27)*.[^cite_note-334]

## Personal life

In a 2018 interview with *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))*, Mbappé spoke about the sacrifices he made as a teenager to focus on his football development: "I did not have the moments of so-called normal people during adolescence, like going out with friends, enjoying good times." He said his life was "totally turned upside down" after he became famous.[^cite_note-Time-335] Just over four years after making his professional debut, Mbappé had over 50 million followers on [Instagram](31591547.md), and by 2023 he had [over 100 million followers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most-followed_Instagram_accounts).[^cite_note-Time-335][^cite_note-336]

Mbappé is a Christian[^cite_note-337] and is fluent in French, English, and Spanish.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] At a press conference during [UEFA Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024), he encouraged French young people to vote against what he called "extreme views" in the [2024 French legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_legislative_election).[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341]

Mbappé is in a relationship with the Spanish actress and model [Ester Expósito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ester_Exp%C3%B3sito).[^cite_note-345]

### Business ventures

Beyond his playing career, Mbappé has built a diversified investment and media portfolio. In 2022, he founded the Los Angeles-based production company *Zebra Valley*, which entered into a multi-year content partnership with the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) later that year.[^cite_note-346] Through his holding company Interconnected Ventures and its investment subsidiary Coalition Capital, he has acquired stakes in several companies, including the fantasy-sports platform [Sorare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorare),[^cite_note-347] the German electronics company [Loewe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loewe_\(electronics\)),[^cite_note-348] and the French health technology company Alan.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350]

On 30 July 2024, Coalition Capital acquired [Oaktree Capital Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oaktree_Capital_Management)'s 80% stake in the [Ligue 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_2) football club [SM Caen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stade_Malherbe_Caen) for a reported €15 million, making Mbappé the youngest majority owner of a professional football club in Europe at the time of the deal.[^cite_note-351]

### Philanthropy

On 22 February 2018, Mbappé and the former African strikers [George Weah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Weah) and [Didier Drogba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didier_Drogba) met with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and [FIFA](11049.md) president [Gianni Infantino](9286117.md) about a sports development project in Africa.[^cite_note-352] Mbappé stated that the development of African sport is important to him due to his parents' African origins.[^cite_note-353]

Following France's victory at the 2018 World Cup, Mbappé donated his prize money to a French charity that helps handicapped people, saying he refused to be paid to represent the French team. He added: "I earn enough money, a lot of money. So I think it's important to help those in need. Many people are suffering, have illnesses. For people like us, lending a hand isn't much. It doesn't change my life, but it changes theirs."[^cite_note-354]

On 28 January 2019, Mbappé donated \$34,000 (roughly £26,000) to a crowdfunding campaign to finance a private search mission to find football player [Emiliano Sala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emiliano_Sala), whose light aircraft had [gone missing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Piper_PA-46_Malibu_crash) over the [English Channel](9230.md) a week earlier.[^cite_note-355] Sala's body was eventually recovered in the wreckage of the aircraft.[^cite_note-356] On 10 February, Mbappé donated £27,000 to fund a search for the plane's pilot, David Ibbotson, who was later found to have also died in the crash.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358]

On 19 November 2021, Mbappé and the Chinese diver [Zhang Jiaqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Jiaqi_\(diver\)) became godfather and godmother, respectively, to two baby pandas at [Beauval Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauval_Zoo), as part of a campaign to raise awareness about the species.[^cite_note-359]
